[Fri Nov 25, 2022 11:54 am](https://gentekpower.com/forums/viewtopic.php?p=22386&sid=70d94c79fc87f5037ef772b7c93fca5a#p22386 "Post")

1st Brand new gen 22kw installed, on NG. 6”wc. 6’ 1” gas line to gen from meter. Ran fine no load , but noticed that that the exhaust manifold was glowing bright red hot during the day. Touch with piece paper that paper or anything touched it went up in flames. While running removing the 1 plug wire from the exhaust manifold hotter side would cause the exhaust flame to stop. the short Under load the gen did not have power to produce & keep up.

checked valve lash gas pressure plugs, basically every test in diagnosis service manual was done, concluded with issues in

following Test performed. Compression cylinder 1-230 cylinder-2 240

leak down failed in cylinder 2 ( assumed exhaust valve issue ) possibly the reason exhaust manifold was getting dangerous fire red.

Warranty/repairs done after contacting gen service with results & videos of the exhaust glow, they sent

new engine, all fuel related components do to the hazard & there recommend to not run gen because dangerous fire hazard.

\- fuel regulator, hoses, complete mixer valve assembly with selector pin & stepper installed.

All new parts were installed and gen seemed to run & produce on & off load as should.

Now were i need help.

The exhaust manifold is still (within 10 seconds, no load & under load ) fire red hot, so hot and bright visible during day. The glow reminds me of how red steel gets if u every cut steel with torch or plasma cutter.

The bright glow starts on both sides of exhaust manifold 2” past were bolts to heads & continues all way past heat shield.

\- ng pressure 6.5”wc off & running

new steppers tested & passed

doing numerous test when running in manual on the upper fuel mixer linkage ( top butter fly ) i manually move the linkage opening the upper butterfly slightly & can notice the exhaust manifold glow starts to go away.

Stepper test performed again & passed

at regulator 1& 2 fuel hose outputs, hose on 1 goes to low fuel in on mixer & 2 on upper input on mixer.

while running I removed the fuel line leaving the regulator @ the mixer on upper input & the gen stayed running, seemed odd,

I removed the fuel line at the mixer on lower input and gen shut down as expected.

I swapped the fuel lines leaving the regulator to the mixer, put regulator out 2 on lower mixer input & out 1 on upper mixer in

Made no difference on situation

I’ve gone through everything top to bottom & really need advice

Trying to find some specs on the operating of how fuel mixer exactly function in details & regulator explaining the 2 output details ext.

Update— I/We install a lot of generac gens, at least 1 a day & lately everyone with 22kw-24kw

Been gen air cooled tech for 7 years

I have noticed when out doing startups on new gens after install completed that I have noticed on just the newer 22kw models with g-force engines 2 out of 4 will have the exhaust manifold glow and the customer always notices with concerns

This does not happen on 16-18-24kws only new model 22kw running on ng

Update— did an start up on one 22kw gen other day and had exhaust manifold glow, when i shut it down i jiggled the fuel line at the mixer upper input, because looked like was not on all way & the metal input fuel port the fuel line slides over on mixer upper port popped right out, did not break just easily came off.

I examined it & didn’t notice any breaks or cracks. I snapped it, pushed it back on & tech support said should be ok if went back in & seemed snug but they are sending an new assembly just incase

Sorry long but this got me stuck & tech support has no answers & are looking into it to see if possible model defect

Help & thanks

happy thanksgiving & holidays

## FULL DIY Generac Generator Maintenance Service - Oil Change ...

**Source:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SkR9Lv1Ex0s

![Thumbnail (1920x1080)](https://i.ytimg.com/vi_webp/SkR9Lv1Ex0s/maxresdefault.webp)
### [FULL DIY Generac Generator Maintenance Service - Oil Change, Air Filter, Spark Plug](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SkR9Lv1Ex0s)

**Visibility**: Public
**Uploaded by**: [Field Guide to DIY](http://www.youtube.com/@FieldGuidetoDIY)
**Uploaded at**: 2024-04-12T16:46:02-07:00
**Published at**: 2024-04-12T16:46:02-07:00
**Length**: 05:06
**Views**: 97924
**Likes**: 1020
**Category**: Howto & Style

#### Description

```
Congratulations! You just made a significant investment in the protection, security, and resale value of your home with the purchase of a whole house backup generator like Generac.

Don’t let your whole house generator break down or its warranty void.  Instead make sure you continue to maintain your whole home generator.

This video demonstrates step by step how to maintain your backup generator by performing you own oil change, oil filter change, air filter change, and spark plug change on this Generac 22kW whole house generator.
Generac Generator Maintenance Kit - https://geni.us/UP6J
Generac Whole House Generator - https://geni.us/HZNxWy
https://amazon.com/shop/FieldGuidetoDIY 
"As an Amazon Influencer, I earn from qualifying purchases.  I also on occasion get products sent for me to review and demonstrate.

SUBSCRIBE for more helpful videos.  https://www.youtube.com/fieldguidetodiy?sub_confirmation=1

Watch these videos for some really good home improvement information:

How to Use JB Weld — https://youtu.be/vRNPNju4ZjU

IKEA Blinds Installation — https://youtu.be/Ig9LoLI6Z0M

Blink Video Doorbell Installation & Setup — https://youtu.be/LkeamdYQHAc

Chapters:
0:00 Introduction
0:17 Let generator run for about 2 minutes
0:24 Stop engine and let it cool
0:33 Turn off rocker switch
0:39 Kill breaker to the house
0:43 Open generator oil fill cap
0:50 Release generator oil drain tube
0:54 Remove oil drain cap and train into drain pan
1:07 Return oil drain cap
1:11 Return generator oil drain tube
1:19 Remove the generator oil filter
1:29 Apply a thin layer of oil to new filter gasket
1:33 Mark the current date on the new oil filter
1:38 Install the new filter
1:50 Begin refilling oil
2:02 Check while refilling (do not overfill)
2:07 Return the oil fill cap
2:15 Remove the air filter cover and the old air filter
2:33 Mark the current date on the new air filter
2:40 Install the new air filter
3:05 Remove the spark plug lead and spark plug
3:20 Install the new spark plug
3:39 Repeat on the rear spark plug
4:11 Return spark plug leads
4:21 Apply a decal with the current date of service
```
